| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Late definition | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/components/test/frameworks-late.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, WebKit (5 tests each). Each framework renders a tag that is defined only afterwards. Angular `[items]`, Vue `.items`, and Svelte (object value) set a property before the upgrade: the element gets the same array, renders its two links, and reports nothing. React 19 and Vue without `.prop` write `items="[object Object],[object Object],[object Object]"`: after the upgrade `items` is unset and `element.attribute.propertyOnly` is reported once; the framework's next render sets the property and the links appear. Observed identically in all three engines before the diagnostic existed |
| Diagnostic | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/core/test/element.browser.test.ts` | Pass in three engines (87 tests): two property-only attributes (`items`, lowercase `renderlabel`) report twice, a normal attribute and a clean element report nothing, and the property keeps its default. The production build has no trace of it: `dist/prod/element/element.js` contains neither the code nor the message, and the counter stays 9,522 B gzip. The first version, a private method called under `DEV`, left the method and its message in production; it is now inline under `if (DEV)` |
| Typed listeners | `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node tests/types packages/components/test/events.types.test.ts packages/analyzer` | Pass. `ElementEvents<typeof Self>` maps `static events` to `CustomEvent<Detail>` (native type for `input`, `change`); 23 elements declare it as `_events`, and `OddkinElement`'s listener overloads read it. Compile-time checks: AI listeners get their detail types, a listener for another detail type and a missing detail field are errors, and a document listener for an AI event gets a plain `Event` (no global entries). `odd-side-nav`'s `static events` keys became the event names. The analyzer reports `analyzer.event.key` for a key that differs from its name once `_events` is declared (fixture `dyn-keyed`), and not for shorthand keys without `_events` (96 analyzer tests) |
| Tag names | `npm run build`, then `tests/jsx/react.types.tsx` | Every `define/*` declaration now starts with `/// <reference path="<element>.d.ts" preserve="true" />` (28 entries), so importing only a registration entry types `HTMLElementTagNameMap`; before, those declarations were `export {}` and `querySelector('odd-side-nav')` was untyped. An `import type {}` was dropped from the emitted declaration, and a type re-export would have added an export to the CEM. CEM and metadata gain no `_events` or `_props` members |
| React JSX | `npx tsc -p tsconfig.jsx.json` (in `npm run typecheck`); `npm run verify:packs` | Pass. `@oddkin/components/jsx/react` (types only) types `odd-side-nav`, `odd-input`, `odd-prompt-input`, and `odd-conversation` with properties (including `renderLabel`), `ref`, React's own attributes and `onInput`/`onChange`, and `on<event>` props with detail types; six expected errors fire (string for `items`, unknown property, wrong detail field twice, undeclared `type` value, read-only `form`). A TSX file in the packed consumer compiles against the installed tarballs with `skipLibCheck: false`. The boundary rules allow React's types only from `src/jsx/` and only in types-only entries (`typeOnly`); 5 checker cases: type import accepted; value import, the same import elsewhere, another package, and a JavaScript target rejected |
| Mutants | see findings | 6 of 6 killed: the property-only check disabled (core test fails); `_events` removed from `odd-prompt-input` (the source test, the listener type test, and the JSX test fail); `on<event>` props typed `any` (two expected JSX errors stop firing); and the boundary cases above for value imports, JavaScript targets, and other packages |
| Pipeline | `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run --project node`, `npx vitest run --project browser`, contract evidence, `node scripts/check-size.mjs`, `npm run verify:packs`, `npx playwright test` | Lint passes except the untracked `.vscode/settings.json`. 601 Node tests. Browser: the first full run failed the two explained-mistake example tests in all engines, because the side-nav `items` and AI `sources` attribute mistakes are now reported (`element.attribute.propertyOnly`); the tests pinned "no runtime report" and were updated. The next full run had 1,865 passed and 28 skipped, with 3 failures under load (Firefox editable list and layout CSS, WebKit keyboard) during repeated screenshot timeouts; each passed alone 3 of 3. e2e 137 passed and 6 skipped, with one WebKit editable-list failure that passed on rerun and 3 of 3 times alone. Metadata evidence 191 + 481 facts; size budgets unchanged |

**Findings:** (1) React 19 and Vue decide property or attribute per render (`name in element`), so data given before a lazy definition loads is silently lost until the next render; the development diagnostic makes it visible, and Angular, Svelte, and Vue `.prop` are unaffected. (2) A class cannot both extend `HTMLElement` and take a second base type redeclaring `addEventListener` (TS2320); `this`-polymorphic overloads on `OddkinElement` plus a type-only `_events` member avoid that and need no per-element runtime code. (3) `HTMLElementEventMap` includes `GlobalEventHandlersEventMap`, which side-nav augments, so filtering "native" events by that map dropped `odd-action`; React 19 adds any lowercase `on<name>` prop on a custom element as a listener, so no filter is needed. (4) The keyboard test failed under full load in Firefox before and WebKit now; it retries twice, and a real mismatch fails every attempt. (5) Core's production `define.js` keeps a bare `import "./element.js"` that esbuild ignores with a warning in size builds; it predates this work (same at HEAD in a separate worktree). (6) Not covered: Vue templates (`vue-tsc`) and Svelte markup (`svelte-check`), neither installed.

**Findings:** (1) The plan sketched development-only freezing. It was replaced by freezing in both builds with a per-store opt-out, because ADR 0031 requires that development builds never change behavior; freezing visits only new objects, so its cost follows the size of the change. | Pipeline | `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run verify:packs`, `npx playwright test e2e/plain-html.spec.ts` | Typecheck, packed consumers (the installed `@oddkin/state/context` imports in Node and its declarations type-check), and the plain-HTML consumer in three engines pass; boundaries, contracts, and CSS compatibility pass; the formatting step fails only on the two files named above |

**Findings:** (1) Engines differ only in `moveBefore()` support; everything else behaves the same in all three. (2) The protocol has no "no longer provided" signal, so a protocol consumer of another implementation keeps its last value when an Oddkin provider stops and nothing else answers; Oddkin consumers fall back and wait. (3) A provider ignores requests from its own host, as Lit's does, which is what lets one element consume a context and provide a derived value of it.

**Findings:** (1) Every engine reports exceeding the Web Storage quota with a `DOMException` whose name contains "quota", thrown synchronously from `setItem`, so the write is the only place it can be caught; the store's update completes first because writes run in a microtask. (2) Restoring is an ordinary store update, so frozen snapshots, inspection, and subscriber semantics apply to restored data without special cases.

**Findings:** (1) Context is what makes an application's root meaningful: consumers below it need no reference to the application, and stop and restart are visible to them through the provider's announcements alone. **Findings:** (1) Two `Scope` behaviors in `@oddkin/core` lose an abort reason: disposing a parent disposes its children without the parent's reason, and a signal first read after `dispose(reason)` is aborted without it. Resources therefore own their request scopes directly and read the owner's signal when they connect, so loads receive the real reason; the core behavior was reported to the core lane, not changed. (2) A stale failure is only observable when it arrives after the current generation settled, so tests must order completions explicitly; a test that only checks the final state can miss a missing guard.

| Commands | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app/test/command.test.ts` | Pass (18 tests). `run()` before `scope.use()` throws `app.command.disconnected` and starts nothing; once connected, the run starts before `run()` returns; `pending` carries the input, attempt 1, and no error; outcomes resolve `success`, `error`, `cancelled`, or `dropped` and never reject; synchronous values and throws settle at once; a failure stays visible with `attempts`, aborts its attempt's signal with the failure, and is not retried without a policy; `cancel()` sets `cancelled` with the input and an `AbortError` (or the given reason, which the attempt's signal also carries), never `idle`, and a later success or failure of that attempt is ignored; the owner's disposal cancels with its own reason, `run()` then throws, and reconnection keeps the state and allows runs; connecting elsewhere cancels the pending run; `exclusive` drops a second run, `queue` runs in call order and continues after a failure, `latest` drops replaced waiting inputs and never aborts the running one; `cancel()` and disconnection drop waiting runs, which never start; a retry policy retries only the failures `when` names, passes `(error, attempt)`, stops after `retries`, and shows the next attempt and the failure while pending; delays (fake timers) wait exactly, a function receives `(attempt, error)`, cancelling during the wait starts nothing, and `scopeStats()` returns to baseline; a throwing `when` and a negative delay are reported as `app.command.retry` and the run fails with its own error; `timeout` fails with a `TimeoutError` that is also the signal's reason, and a policy may retry it; `reset()` clears settled states only; `dispose()` cancels and refuses reconnection; signals read by `run` never become dependencies of the calling effect; a run that cancels its own command, and a nested `run()`, are well defined; invalid options throw `app.command.options` |
| Cart example | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/app/test/command.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (2 tests). The §8.1 element: Add is disabled while the cart loads and while an addition is pending; a 503 is retried with the same item after the policy delay and the status shows "Retrying Tea (attempt 2)"; a 409 is not retried and the optimistic item is rolled back; Cancel aborts the request, keeps the item, reads the cart again, and the item the server added anyway stays; removing the element aborts the pending request with a reason naming the element, sets `cancelled`, and returns `scopeStats()` to baseline; reconnecting reads the cart again; no `unhandledrejection` event fired. The first Chromium run failed: the test removed and re-appended the element in one task, so the cancelled addition's continuation ran after reconnection and its `cart.refresh()` superseded the request the test answered. The test now lets the continuation run while disconnected (finding 1) |
| Production build | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app` | Pass (52 tests, including the production build): `app.command.disconnected` and `app.command.options` keep their codes in `dist/prod` with the generic message, and the development explanations are absent from `dist/prod`; the root entry re-exports `./command` |
| Mutants | `.tmp/mutate.mjs` (one mutant at a time; original bytes restored and hash-checked) | 28 of 28 killed: `run()` allowed without an owner; the first attempt started in a microtask; `exclusive` not dropping; `queue` as the default; `latest` keeping every waiting input; cancellation returning to `idle` (killed in Node and in the Chromium cart example); late successes and late failures after cancellation committing; cancellation not aborting the attempt or the retry wait; cancellation, owner disposal, or failure aborting without the real reason; one retry more than allowed; `when` ignored; the delay ignored; the wait showing the failed attempt's number; a throwing policy not reported; an invalid computed delay accepted; the timeout never armed; the queue stopping after a settled run; `reset()` clearing a pending run; connecting elsewhere keeping the run; a disposed command reconnecting; `run` tracked by the calling effect; a settled error counting one attempt; `retry` without `when` accepted; disconnection not cancelling (Chromium cart example). The retry wait's own guard was not probed: cancelling disposes the wait's scope and clears its timer, so removing the guard is equivalent |
| Size | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | `command` 3,723 B gzip with `Scope`, the reactive graph, and diagnostics (budget 4,096 B); a graph guard keeps rendering modules, `@oddkin/state`, and the resource and application modules out of it |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node packages/state packages/app tests/architecture`; `npx vitest run --project browser packages/state packages/app`; `npm run verify:packs`; `npx playwright test e2e/plain-html.spec.ts`; `npm run lint:contracts` | Pass: typecheck including tests and framework typings; 128 Node tests; 71 browser tests and 1 skip (WebKit `moveBefore()`); packed consumers import `@oddkin/app/command`; the plain-HTML consumer loads the app root, which now re-exports `command`, in three engines; contracts up to date (§8.1 is outside the contract markers) |

**Findings:** (1) Runs belong to a connection, so moving an element (removal and insertion in one task) cancels its pending mutation; a continuation that re-reads after `cancelled` then reads on the new connection, which is the safe outcome, but a test or application that answers requests by position must account for it. (2) A cancelled read and a cancelled mutation need different terminal states: a resource returns to its prior state, because a read changed nothing, while a command stays `cancelled`, because its effect on the server is unknown. Separating `dropped` (never started) from `cancelled` (started) is what lets a consumer roll back only when the change certainly did not happen.

## 2026-10-19 — Router core in @oddkin/app (M7, §4.1)

Environment as the previous entry. Another agent was editing other files in the same working tree; `npm run lint` passed the contract check, the build and boundary check (the new `./router` entry imports in Node without DOM globals), and the CSS check, and failed only at `prettier --check` on that agent's three in-progress analyzer files; `npx prettier --check` on this slice's files passes. Contract: [core-contracts.md §9](./architecture/core-contracts.md#9-application-root-and-router-oddkinapp).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Matching and options | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app/test/router.test.ts` | Pass (10 tests): static, parameter, index, and rest patterns per level; a `params` function returning `undefined` declines the match and the next route is tried; siblings in definition order; a parent ends the match when no child matches the empty rest; raw params accumulate across levels and are decoded, undecodable paths match nothing, and empty segments are ignored; a throwing `params` propagates; invalid patterns throw `app.router.route` from `route()` and, for plain objects at any depth, from `createRouter()`; invalid `base`, `mode`, and `routes` throw `app.router.options`; `href()` writes the base path or the fragment and resolves relative paths, and other origins throw `app.router.path`; `navigate()` and `refresh()` throw `app.router.disconnected` before the router runs, and connecting in Node throws `app.router.environment` |
| Router in a document | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/app/test/router.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (9 tests), with the real History API. Nested levels commit from the URL; a navigation pushes, `replace` replaces, the kept outer level is not loaded again and keeps its match object, the left level's scope ends with "router left …"; a declined parameter is not-found; titles come from the deepest level. A pending navigation shows `navigating` while the URL stays; superseding it aborts its load ("router navigation superseded"), its late result never commits, one history entry is added, and `scopeStats()` returns to baseline. A failing nested level keeps its parent `ready`; `refresh()` loads it again; a failed `import()`, a throwing `search`, and a throwing `params` are errors. Back and forward restore locations; a new query reloads only the level that declares `search`; a fragment change reloads nothing. Links in light and open shadow DOM, `target="_self"`, relative links, the base path without its slash, and the current URL (replace) are intercepted; Ctrl, Meta, Shift, Alt, the middle button, `target="_blank"`, `download`, `rel="external"`, outside the base path, another origin, a page fragment, and a click the page already handled are not. Redirects replace on start and push for a link, and a loop ends in `app.router.redirect`. Hash mode keeps the path in the fragment, an in-page fragment link keeps the route (`#/other#details`), and a fragment set directly is rewritten in full. `formatTitle` composes titles and not-found; a throwing title is reported once. As an application service, `stop(reason)` ends every level with that reason, removes the listeners (a later click is not intercepted), and returns `scopeStats()` to baseline; a restart reads the URL again. The first Chromium run failed one assertion: the test's relative link `products/3` from `/app/products/2` resolves to `/app/products/products/3`, as URLs do; the test now uses `3` |
| Production build | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app` | Pass (63 tests, including the production build): `app.router.route`, `app.router.options`, and `app.router.disconnected` keep their codes in `dist/prod` with the generic message; the router's development explanations are absent from `dist/prod`; the root entry re-exports `./router` |
| Mutants | `.tmp/mutate.mjs` (one mutant at a time; original bytes restored and hash-checked) | 38 of 38 behavior mutants killed: each link escape removed in turn (Ctrl, button, `download`, `target`, `rel="external"`, origin, base path, page fragment, already handled, `composedPath()` replaced by the event target); superseded loads committing; no level kept; levels kept regardless of params; levels that declare `search` kept on a new query, or every level reloaded; left or superseded levels not disposed; async loads not awaited; `navigating` not shown; redirects always replacing; no redirect limit; stop without the application's reason; the outermost title winning; `formatTitle` ignored; title failures unreported; a hash-mode page fragment treated as a route; the full fragment not written; `popstate` ignored; same-URL links pushing; `replace` ignored; `refresh()` keeping levels; `/app` not recognized; a failing `search` counted as ready; a throwing `params` counted as not-found; and four matcher mutants (declined params, a one-segment rest, a parent never ending the match, undecoded segments). A first version also listened to `hashchange` in hash mode; the mutant that removed that listener survived in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit, so it was removed (finding 1) |
| Size | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | `router` 5,461 B gzip with `Scope`, the reactive graph, and diagnostics (budget 6,144 B; 5,511 B before the `hashchange` listener was removed); a graph guard keeps rendering modules, `@oddkin/state`, and the resource and command modules out of it |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node packages/state packages/app tests/architecture`; `npx vitest run --project browser packages/state packages/app`; `npm run verify:packs`; `npx playwright test e2e/plain-html.spec.ts` | Pass: typecheck (the first run failed because two test parsers that only throw were inferred as `never`, and `Route<never, …>` is not assignable to `AnyRoute`; their return types are now annotated); 139 Node tests; 98 browser tests and 1 skip (WebKit `moveBefore()`); packed consumers import `@oddkin/app/router` in Node; the plain-HTML consumer loads the app root, which now re-exports the router, in three engines |

**Findings:** (1) `popstate` fires for fragment navigations, including `location.hash = …`, in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit at the tested versions, so hash mode needs no `hashchange` listener. (2) Writing the history entry when a navigation commits, rather than when it starts, is what lets a superseded navigation leave no entry; while loading, the address bar keeps the committed location and `state.navigating` names the pending one. Back and forward are the exception: the browser has already changed the URL. (3) The link escapes were verified by recording the router's decision and then preventing the default action in a window listener, because a real new tab or download would leave the test page; the browsers' own handling of those escapes is not exercised by these tests. (4) Focus after navigation, route announcements, and scroll restoration are not implemented yet.

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |

Host attributes, part tags and classes, and states (`enabled` for `:enabled` and `:not([aria-disabled='true'])`, `disabled`, `hover`, `active`, `focus-visible`, `[aria-x='v']`), deduplicated and sorted, with their specificity. Unread selectors and what they may style: other combinators, `:not(…)` lists, data attributes, `:host(:hover)`, `:host-context()`, universal and element-less selectors, and elements with a computed class list. Pseudo-elements and slotted content style no part. The winner by order, specificity, `!important`, and layers (a later layer beats specificity, unlayered beats layered, `!important` reverses both); a winner without a token removes the mapping. `@media`, `@supports`, and `@container` are stated in `when`; `@keyframes` and `@starting-style` are skipped; `@scope` is unread; braces in strings and comments are ignored; nested style rules and computed selectors leave no mappings |
| Published metadata | `npx vitest run --project node packages/components/test/metadata.test.ts packages/ai/test/metadata.test.ts` | Pass. `odd-button` maps its default, `appearance`, `size`, hover (under `@media (hover: hover)`), active, and disabled styles to tokens; `odd-side-nav`'s entries have no colour mapping, because a forced-colours rule (`.root .entry:disabled`) is outside the grammar; `focusRingStyles` publishes its `css`. `odd-ai-label`'s host `font-size` is `font.size.xs` only: its own sheet loads after `aiElementStyles` (`font.size.md`), read after three sheets published by `@oddkin/components`. `button` styles both `odd-suggestions` buttons and `.send` outranks it on one |
| Golden fixture | `UPDATE_FIXTURES=1 npx vitest run --project node packages/analyzer/test/analyzer.test.ts` | core-counter's two style sources gain `css`; they read no `token()`, so there are no mappings |
| Counts | `node scripts/build-metadata.mjs` | 4 components with 120 mappings and 24 AI elements with 248. Published `css` adds 17,014 B of CSS text to the `@oddkin/components` metadata (13 sources) and 33,836 B to `@oddkin/ai` (25 sources) |
| Mutants | `.tmp/mutate-sm.mjs` (one at a time, with an analyzer and metadata rebuild for `extract.ts` and `emit.ts` mutants; original bytes restored and checked) | 21 of 21 killed: a cascade tie keeping the earlier declaration; unread rules not blocking mappings; `!important` keeping normal layer order; `!important` ignored; at-rule conditions dropped; universal selectors styling nothing; computed class lists ignored; a child combinator read as a descendant; unknown at-rules read as transparent; nested style rules read; keyframes and starting styles read as steady state; strings not skipped; states neither deduplicated nor sorted; states adding no specificity; computed selectors read; pseudo-elements read as the part; published CSS ignored; only the template node scanned for parts; a tag selecting only the last part rendered with it; published style entries without CSS. Three survived at first (nested rules, keyframes, computed selectors) because each test case also failed for another reason; each now has a case only that rule decides |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npx vitest run --project node`, `npm run verify:packs`, `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | Pass: typecheck (with the framework typings), lint (contracts, boundaries with built artifacts, CSS compatibility, formatting), 738 Node tests in 73 files, packed consumers of all eight packages, and every size budget |
| Verified facts | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/components/test/contract.browser.test.ts packages/ai/test/contract.browser.test.ts`, then `node scripts/build-metadata.mjs` and `node scripts/verify-metadata.mjs` | `verify:metadata` first failed: no contract-test record matched the current build. After both suites ran in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (672 passed, 24 skipped), it attaches 191 verified facts for 4 components and 481 for 24 AI elements, as before; style mappings stay `extracted` |

**Findings:** (1) The first draft read every rule independently: `odd-ai-label`'s host font size had two tokens (`font.size.md` from the shared AI sheet and `font.size.xs` from its own), a `button` rule was attributed to one of two buttons, and rules inside `@media (hover: hover)` lost their condition. Mappings now follow the cascade and state their at-rule conditions. (2) Sheets imported from another package published only the tokens they read, so `@oddkin/ai`'s mappings could not be determined; each package now publishes every exported sheet's CSS text, and a source without text leaves the component without mappings. (3) Parts rendered by helpers beside a template (such as `odd-side-nav`'s badge) were invisible to the template node; the whole template file is read.

Environment as the previous entry. Another agent was working in the same tree, and during the e2e runs the CPU was at 100% with 26 Node processes. `npm run lint` passed the contract check, the build and boundary check, and the CSS check, and failed only at `prettier --check` on that agent's three in-progress analyzer files; `npx prettier --check` on this slice's files passes. Contract: [core-contracts.md §9](./architecture/core-contracts.md#9-application-root-and-router-oddkinapp).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| After a navigation | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/app/test/router-after.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (5 tests). The first load moves no focus and announces nothing; a navigation to another path focuses the application's target (an `h1` gets `tabindex="-1"`, a button gets none) and announces the title in a polite live region; query and fragment changes keep focus and announce nothing; back to another path focuses and announces again. A custom `announce` message is used; `announce: false` adds no region; regions are removed on stop; throwing `focus` and `announce` are reported (`app.router.focus`, `app.router.announce`). A new path starts at the top; back and forward restore 1,200 px and 500 px; `#deep` is scrolled into view; a new query and `refresh()` keep the position; after a native fragment navigation, back returns to the 800 px the page was left at. `history.scrollRestoration` is `manual` while running and given back (`auto`) on stop; `scroll: false` leaves it and the position alone. Two navigations from the top write exactly two `pushState` calls and no `replaceState`; a scroll pause writes one `replaceState` with the position. The first runs failed in WebKit with `SecurityError: Attempt to use history.pushState() more than 100 times per 10 seconds` (finding 1); the `scroll: false` test failed in Chromium only after other tests, because with `auto` restoration Chromium reset the position to 0 at the next frame (the test now checks that `auto` is left alone, then switches to `manual` as an application would) |
| Routing and refused writes | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/app/test/router.browser.test.ts`; `npx vitest run --project node packages/app/test/router.test.ts` | Pass: 9 tests in three engines (unchanged, now on the shared `router-fixture.ts`) and 11 Node tests, including a fake window whose `pushState` throws WebKit's `SecurityError`: the navigation resolves `false`, the committed location stays, the new level's signal is aborted with that error, `app.router.history` is reported once, and `scrollRestoration` is set and given back |
| Real browsers | `npx playwright test e2e/router.spec.ts e2e/plain-html.spec.ts` | `examples/router` (history mode below `/examples/router/`, and `hash.html` in hash mode), through an import map with no bundler: links navigate in one document with titles, focus on the `h1`, and the announced title; browser back and forward restore 1,200 px and the top; a fragment link scrolls to its section; a legacy route redirects; a `target="_blank"` link opens another page; a `download` link downloads `app.js`; a `rel="external"` link inside the base path loads `hash.html` as a new document; a reload returns to 900 px once the position is stored in the entry; hash mode reloads a deep link and keeps the route for in-page fragments. A real Ctrl+click opened another page in Chromium and WebKit while the document stayed and the click was not prevented. Final spec: one run passed fully (20 passed, 1 skipped); two runs each had one Firefox failure from a 30 s timeout creating the page in `beforeEach` under the full CPU load; Firefox alone with one worker passed (5 passed, 1 skipped). Earlier versions: in Firefox a Ctrl+click's page was not reported within 5 s in 2 of 4 runs and the page once stopped responding, so that test is skipped in Firefox (the router's decision is covered there by the fixtures); a combined tabs, download, and external-link test timed out twice in Firefox at the link after the download, so the download is now a test of its own. The plain-HTML consumer passes in three engines with `@oddkin/core/controllers/announce` added to its import map |
| Mutants | `.tmp/mutate.mjs` (one mutant at a time; original bytes restored and hash-checked) | 23 of 23 killed: focus not moved; an unfocusable target not made focusable; a focusable one given a tabindex; a new query moving focus; the first load moving focus; announcements sharing one key; a custom message ignored; no live regions; focus and announce failures unreported; restoration left `auto` or not given back; a new path keeping its position; positions never restored; fragments not scrolled to; a pop recording the live position (killed in Firefox and WebKit, equivalent in Chromium, finding 3); positions left by a pop forgotten; unchanged positions written again; positions never stored when scrolling pauses; refused URLs unreported or leaving new levels running. On first runs, "restoration not given back" survived because the test compared with a value an earlier test had already left `manual` (the test now sets `auto` first), and one mutant's run ended without a test summary and was killed on a rerun. An update of the last position after the router's own scrolling mattered only for a traversal within the same frame, could not be tested, and was removed |
| Size | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | `router` 6,974 B gzip (budget raised from 6,144 B to 7,680 B; 5,461 B before this slice). Core's `LiveAnnouncer` alone is 735 B gzip and is imported even with `announce: false`; esbuild already shortens private names |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node packages/state packages/app tests/architecture tests/examples`; `npx vitest run --project browser packages/state packages/app`; `npm run verify:packs` | Pass: typecheck; 223 Node tests; 113 browser tests and 1 skip (WebKit `moveBefore()`); packed consumers |

**Findings:** (1) WebKit refuses more than 100 `pushState`/`replaceState` calls in 10 seconds per document and throws `SecurityError`, so a router must keep history writes few and survive a refused one: a navigation from the top writes one entry, a scroll pause one replacement, and a refused URL abandons the navigation instead of leaving the view and the URL apart. The router tests run in two documents to stay under the limit. (2) A position written into `history.state` during `pagehide` survives a reload only in WebKit; Chromium and Firefox dropped it (`history.state` was `null` after the reload in both), so positions are stored once scrolling pauses. (3) Firefox and WebKit scroll to a fragment before firing `popstate`, Chromium after it, so the position an entry was left at must come from the last `scroll` event, not from `scrollY` at `popstate`. (4) Under heavy load, Playwright's Firefox was the only engine to time out creating pages and to lose track of pages opened by a Ctrl+click; these are recorded as environment limits, not router failures.

**Findings:** (1) V8 rolls impossible dates over (`Date.parse('2026-02-30')` is 2 March), so the calendar check compares the round-tripped ISO date. (2) In a shallow clone `git log` reports the clone's only commit as every file's last change; the analyzer omits dates there instead. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` checks out with the default depth of 1, so CI-built metadata carries no dates unless a release build fetches full history (M12). (3) Oddkin's build names the theme sources (`packages/tokens/themes/default.theme.json`, `packages/tokens/src/generate/preset.ts`) and fails if either moves, so a foundation page never silently loses its date.

The first run left 3 survivors: two pattern guards that the test cases also failed through other checks (cases changed so each guard is tested alone; both then killed), and a fallback lookup of enrichment by tag that never changed the outcome (removed; lookup now by tag only, as the registry does). The remaining survivor, looking enrichment up by key instead of tag, is equivalent under the metadata schema, whose component keys are `component:<tag>`. |
| Pipeline | `npm run lint`; `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node`; `node scripts/check-size.mjs`; `npm run verify:packs` | Lint passes (contracts current; boundaries OK, including `./data` importing in Node without DOM globals; formatting). Typecheck: errors only in another agent's untracked page-lifecycle files (`e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts`, `packages/app/test/lifecycle.browser.test.ts`). Node suite: all Workbench tests pass. In a full run under three agents' load, analyzer, token-schema, and typed-listener tests timed out; rerun alone they pass. Two `packages/app` tests on the in-progress page lifecycle fail, alone too, and are not affected by this change. Size: all budgets pass. Packed consumers: OK, `@oddkin/workbench` included. |

**Findings:** (1) The analyzer emits `category:<slug>` from `@category` but no `categories` records with labels, so the registry puts every real component in "Other components" with `pages.category.unknown`. This is handled in the catalog slice (item 822), with a request to the analyzer's owner for label records. (2) The index and page files are an internal format between the Node tooling and the shell of one Workbench version, versioned by `format` and `version` rather than a published schema.

## 2026-10-20 — Page suspension and restoration in @oddkin/app (M7 item 899, §4.1 item 218)

Environment as the previous entries (Windows, Node 24, Playwright 1.64 with its Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit builds). Other agents were editing `packages/core` (Scope reasons: `scope.ts`, its tests, `CONTRACTS.md`, `contracts.json`, core-contracts.md §4, and the analyzer fixture those hashes feed) and `packages/workbench` (data and Node entries) in the same working tree; this slice's shared-file edits were committed against HEAD only, without their hunks. | Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cache probes | `node .tmp/bfcache/probe*.mjs` (two plain pages, link away, `goBack`) | Under Playwright's defaults no engine restored a page (`pageshow.persisted` false, a new heap): Playwright launches Chromium with `--disable-back-forward-cache`, its Firefox sets `fission.bfcacheInParent` false and disables the cache in its driver, and its WebKit never restored. Chromium without that switch: the headless shell refuses (`BackForwardCacheDisabledForDelegate`, `BrowsingInstanceNotSwapped` from CDP's `Page.backForwardCacheNotUsed`; `notRestoredReasons` only says `masked`), while the full build in new headless (`channel: 'chromium'`) and headed restores, with the same heap, `pagehide`/`pageshow` persisted, and `freeze`/`resume`. Firefox with the preference overridden: `goBack` timed out and the driver lost the restored page. WebKit headed: not restored. CDP `Page.setWebLifecycleState` (frozen, then active) fired neither `freeze` nor `resume` in headless Chromium, and another tab in front did not hide the page |
| Lifecycle and policy | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app/test/lifecycle.test.ts` | Pass (16 tests, fake window). `app.suspend()` calls services in reverse order of `use()`, `resume()` in order; both are idempotent and no-ops while stopped; the run's scope, signal, listeners, and timers are untouched (`scopeStats()` equal). A throwing service is reported (`app.service.suspend`/`resume`, origin the app name) and the others are called. `suspend()`/`resume()` from a hook are rejected (`app.lifecycle.reentrant`, reported); `stop()` inside a pass ends it; a service used while suspended is suspended at once; a stop clears the suspension. `pageLifecycle` registers `pagehide`, `pageshow`, `visibilitychange`, `freeze`, `resume` once (only the first three without `onfreeze` or with `freeze: false`); three cache round trips suspend and resume three times with nothing registered again; non-persisted transitions do nothing; Chromium's overlap (`pagehide`, `freeze`, `resume`, `pageshow`) resumes once, at `pageshow`, and a restoration also ends a freeze that had no `resume`; a manual suspension survives visibility changes; a stop removes every listener and later events reach nothing; no window gives `app.lifecycle.environment`. Resources: only `revalidateOnRestore` reloads; a success younger than `freshFor` is kept, an error is reloaded however young the success, an older one is reloaded and its failure surfaces as `error` with `previous`; a load pending across the suspension is aborted and its late result never wins; idle, disconnected, connected-elsewhere, and service-less cases load nothing extra; one restoration counts once for two applications even when effects flush between their listeners; a pending command is not run again |
| Real document | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/app/test/lifecycle.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (3 tests), with real `PageTransitionEvent` objects. Importing all 31 runtime entries of core, components (with the four `define/*` registrations), state, and app and connecting `odd-card`, `odd-input`, `odd-button`, and `odd-side-nav` registers no lifecycle listener on the window or document. An application with the service registers `pagehide`, `pageshow`, `visibilitychange` (and `freeze`, `resume` in Chromium) once; three round trips pause and resume a poller's interval (timers −1 while suspended, no ticks; `scopeStats()` equal after); `stop()` returns `scopeStats()` to baseline. An element whose resource has the policy reloads only while a service runs and while connected. The first run recorded no window listeners: the test runner gives `window` its own `addEventListener`, which bypasses a patch of `EventTarget.prototype`; the recorder now wraps it |
| Real restoration | `npx playwright test e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts` | 14 passed, 4 skipped. Chromium (its own full-build browser without the switch): two restorations in a row keep the document (`loadId`), the count, and `scopeStats()`, add no listener, give the audit sequence `pagehide:true`, `service:suspend`, `freeze`, `resume`, `pageshow:true`, `service:resume` each time, and reload only the resource with the policy (`load 2`, then `load 3`, the other stays `load 1`); after an explicit stop a restoration resumes and reloads nothing and no lifecycle listener remains. All three engines: one `pagehide`/`pageshow`/`visibilitychange` listener (plus `freeze`/`resume` in Chromium) and no `unload` or `beforeunload`; a reload is a new document with `pageshow:false` and nothing resumed; `pushState` plus back fires no `pagehide`/`pageshow`; stop removes the listeners and start adds them once (`scopeStats()` equal to the first run). Firefox and WebKit skip the two real-restoration tests as not verified. One three-engine run timed out closing the second Chromium after 30 s in `afterAll` under load (3.6–4.6 s otherwise); the hook now has 60 s |
| Production build | `npx vitest run --project node packages/app` | Pass (81 tests): `app.lifecycle.reentrant` and `app.lifecycle.environment` keep their codes in `dist/prod`, their development explanations are absent, and the root entry re-exports `./lifecycle` |
| Mutants | `.tmp/mutate.mjs`; `.tmp/mutate-e2e.mjs` (mutate, rebuild `packages/app`, run Playwright, restore, rebuild) | 38 of 38 killed: 34 against the Node and browser tests (app order, idempotency, flags, error isolation, reentrancy, stopped passes, late services; lifecycle persisted checks, counting, noting, freeze gating and option, resume, visibility, manual suspension, state after stop; per-event dedupe; resource policy, freshness, error freshness, idle, old connections, first run) and 4 against the real-browser e2e (restoration not noted, suspension not at `pagehide`, Chromium's `resume` ending a stay, an extra `unload` listener). First runs left three survivors that strengthened tests: the per-event dedupe (synthetic dispatch coalesced the counts; a listener that flushes between the applications' listeners now stands in for a browser's microtask checkpoint), an error counted as fresh (first reported killed from a run that failed without a summary; a clean rerun survived; the test now fails a refresh while the success is young), and suspension not at `pagehide` in real Chromium (its `freeze` suspended anyway; the example's service now logs its hooks into the audit sequence). A redundant `!active` check and a counter equivalent to a first-run flag were removed instead of probed |
| Size | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | `lifecycle` 4,676 B gzip with the application module (budget 5,120 B, new; a graph guard keeps rendering modules, the store, persistence, router, resource, and command code out of it). `app` 4,975 B (budget raised from 5,120 B to 5,376 B; +208 B for `suspend()`/`resume()`), `resource` 3,527 B (+111 B for the policy; budget 3,840 B). `command` and `router` grew by 12 and 14 B without changes here, from the other agent's in-progress core `Scope` change compiled by `tsc -b` |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node packages/state packages/app tests/architecture tests/examples`; `npx vitest run --project browser packages/state packages/app`; `npm run lint`; `npm run verify:packs`; `npx playwright test e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts e2e/router.spec.ts e2e/plain-html.spec.ts` | Typecheck passes (the first run caught two type errors in this slice's tests). 243 Node tests pass, including public imports of `examples/lifecycle`. 122 browser tests pass, 1 skipped (WebKit `moveBefore()`). Lint: contracts, boundaries (the `./lifecycle` entry imports in Node without DOM globals), and CSS pass; `prettier --check` fails only on two of the other agent's in-progress workbench files. Packed consumers pass. e2e: Chromium and WebKit pass; in the three-engine run 4 Firefox tests failed (three timeouts creating or closing pages, with Firefox `RenderCompositorSWGL` graphics errors in the log, and one router scroll poll), and Firefox alone with one worker passed all of them (10 passed, 3 skipped) |

**Findings:** (1) A real back/forward cache restoration can be tested only in Chromium here, and only with the full build and without Playwright's `--disable-back-forward-cache`; Playwright's Firefox and WebKit cannot restore pages from the cache, so those engines stay not verified, and a test that merely navigates back proves nothing there. (2) Chromium's order is `pagehide` (persisted), `freeze`, then `resume`, `pageshow` (persisted). Suspending at `pagehide` gives services a chance to close connections before the page is frozen, and treating `resume` as the end of a stay would resume services while the page is still being restored. (3) A browser runs microtasks between the listeners of a native event, so effects can flush between two applications' listeners; restorations must be counted once per event, which synthetic `dispatchEvent` in tests does not reveal by itself. (4) A mutation run that fails without a test summary is not a kill; rerun it before counting it.

Packages are found up the tree, breadth first in manifest order; a nested copy serves its dependent and both copies appear; a linked package resolves to its real directory once; missing documented packages are errors and missing dependencies warnings (optional peers skipped); unsafe names are never paths. The shell's map excludes `@oddkin/analyzer` and TypeScript. |

The built command in a real Node process serves an Acme project from its TypeScript config, and refuses a config with an `enum` (Node's type stripping rejects it) with the hint. |

One `find` text did not match the formatted source (fixed, then killed). One survivor, `findPackageDir` without `realpathSync`, was hidden by `readPackage` resolving real paths again; a test now pins the documented real path, and it is killed. |

**Findings:** (1) Passing package records instead of manifests to `createImportMap` type-checked, because every manifest field is optional, and gave each package a bare prefix mapping. A smoke run against the real components caught it before any test existed; the import-map traversal test now guards it. (2) Under Vitest, a dynamic `import()` of a `.ts` file goes through Vite's transform, which accepts any TypeScript, so Node's type-stripping limits are verified only through the built command in a separate process. (3) Package URLs carry the version (`_oddkin/m/<name>@<version>/`), because the shell and the documented library may load different copies of one package. (4) Not done in this slice: `--analyze` (rerunning the analyzer in the dev loop), the shell page itself (`@oddkin/workbench/app` is the next slice; until then the served page's module fails to load), and frame documents.

Environment as the previous entries. Other agents were editing `packages/core` (Scope reasons) and `packages/workbench` (`./node`, the `oddkin` bin, and packages.md's Workbench row) in the same working tree; this slice's shared-file edits were committed against HEAD only. Agent 3 asked for the API in `.tmp/lanes.md`; the answer there names the entries and functions below. Contracts: [theming-and-styling.md §2, §8, §9](./architecture/theming-and-styling.md); [workbench.md §6.2, §6.4](./architecture/workbench.md).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Probes | `node .tmp/probe-hostile-drafts.mjs`; `node .tmp/probe-schema-agreement.mjs`; `node .tmp/probe-schema-paths.mjs` | No JSON draft made `generate` throw (53 hostile drafts: wrong top-level and nested types, prototype-named keys). 13 of 90 top-level drafts were accepted by the generator but rejected by the schema: an explicit `null` for `name`, `status`, `status.danger`, `spacing`, `modes`, `products`, `density`, or `overrides` was read as "absent", and `$schema` was never type-checked. Errors in override values were reported at `/tokens/<name>`, which a draft editor cannot point at; profile values already used their configuration path. |
| Generator | `npx vitest run --project node packages/tokens` | Pass (81 tests, 6 new). An explicit `null` gives `config.type`, `config.name.invalid`, `modes.invalid`, or `color.invalid` at its own path, and a non-string `$schema` gives `config.type`. An override's alias, cycle, tier, CSS, unit, floor-syntax, and target-size problems are at `/overrides/<name>`; a role that fails because of the override keeps `/tokens/<role>`, density failures keep `/density/<name>/tokens/<role>`, and contrast warnings name the foreground role. 26 hostile drafts give `ok: false` without throwing, and each error path, or its parent, exists in the draft. The schema test replaces every value of three complete configurations with each of 8 wrong-typed values (584 drafts): none is accepted by the generator and rejected by the schema. The 16 reverse cases are semantic checks the schema cannot express: units, the names count, and missing aliases. The committed CSS fixtures, `css/default.css`, `inspect/default.json`, and `harbor.css` are unchanged. |
| Loader | same (`cli.test.ts`) | `loadConfig` returns the JSON-compatible model. `undefined` properties are dropped, `-0` becomes `0`, a shared value is copied, and a JSON `__proto__` key stays an own key, which the generator rejects (`config.unknownKey /__proto__`). A Map, an array hole, `NaN`, a function, a `bigint`, `undefined` in an array, and a cycle are seven `config.notJson` errors at `/products`, `/modes/1`, `/density/compact/scale`, `/density/tight/scale`, `/overrides/radius.md`, `/status/0`, and `/self/config`; `check` exits 1. |
| Worker | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/tokens` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (27 tests per engine, 4 new). A module Worker imports the published `@oddkin/tokens/generate` and answers drafts by `postMessage`. For `themes/default.theme.json`, its CSS and inspection text equal `dist/css/default.css` and `dist/inspect/default.inspect.json` byte for byte, read with `commands.readFile`. For `examples/tokens-css-only/theme.json`, its CSS equals the `harbor.css` that the real `oddkin-tokens` bin wrote with `--strict`. Invalid drafts come back with configuration paths, suggestions, and no CSS, and the Worker keeps answering. A draft with a contrast warning comes back `ok` with CSS. In a frame document, a sheet constructed with the frame's `CSSStyleSheet` holds the same rules as a `<style>` parse; adopted, then replaced with `replaceSync`, it changes `--odd-radius-md` from `0.375rem` to `0.625rem` with the same nodes. Adopting a sheet constructed in the parent document throws; afterwards the array is empty in Chromium and Firefox (the token is unset) and unchanged in WebKit. One three-engine run produced no results: Firefox failed to connect to its browser session and a tester iframe was not ready within 60 s, with a reported duration of 38,651 s (a clock jump, likely the machine sleeping). Each engine alone, then all three together, passed. |
| Mutants | `node .tmp/mutate.mjs` | 20 probes, 19 killed, 1 equivalent. Killed: `null` read as absent, the `$schema` check, the status guard (a TypeError for `null`), unmapped override paths (with both versions of the guard), unmapped graph or target-size paths, and in `loadConfig`: non-finite numbers, `-0`, functions and `bigint`, the cycle check, shared values taken for cycles, holes copied or reported at the array, class instances, `undefined` properties, `__proto__` assigned, and a partial model returned. A Worker that posts unlayered CSS was killed, and so was a change to the built `dist/generate/css.js`: the Worker test exercises the published entry. The survivor, mapping any provenance and not only configuration pointers, is equivalent. Generated tokens record formulas, but none can reach a base-graph or target-size diagnostic: their values are literal colours and dimensions, only preset roles declare minimums, and cycles pass through the overridden reference first. The guard now tests for a pointer, which states that intent. |
| Pipeline | `npm run lint`; `npm run typecheck`; `npx vitest run --project node`; `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | Lint passes: contracts; boundaries with built artifacts (384 files; no Node built-in in the tokens browser entries); CSS; formatting. Typecheck passes. Node suite: 837 tests in 80 files pass. Size: 26 budgets pass. |

**Findings:** (1) Treating `null` as "absent" made the generator more lenient than its own schema, so an editor validating against the schema and Theme Studio validating with the generator would disagree. The property test now keeps them in one direction: the generator is at least as strict. (2) A rejected `adoptedStyleSheets` assignment is not atomic in Chromium and Firefox; it empties the array. Theme Studio must construct preview sheets in the frame's realm and update them with `replaceSync`, never by assigning a foreign sheet. (3) A user profile that redefines a preset profile keeps the preset's position, so an overlap warning can point at the preset profile's path (`/profiles/layout.compact/…`), which is not in the draft. This is left for the §5.1 scope-composition work (item 244). (4) The generated CSS selects `:root` and scope attributes, so adopted in a shadow root its `:root` rules match nothing; preview frames adopt the theme in the document.

## 2026-10-21 — `Scope` keeps its disposal reason (core-contracts.md §4)

Environment: a replacement machine after the previous one failed. Windows, Node 24.22.1, npm 11.19.1, Playwright 1.64.1 (Chrome for Testing 156.0.8078.4, Firefox 157.0, WebKit 27.2). The fix was written on 2026-10-20 in Agent 1's lane and committed unverified in `1b080f6`; this entry verifies it. Reported by Agent 2 (`.tmp/lanes.md`). Contract: [core-contracts.md §4](./architecture/core-contracts.md).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before | Agent 2's report: `const s = createScope(); s.dispose(new Error('x')); s.signal.reason` | The signal, allocated lazily and first read after `dispose(reason)`, aborted with a generic `AbortError`, and children disposed with their parent got no reason. `@oddkin/app` resources, commands, and the router read `scope.signal` eagerly to work around it. |
| Unit (DOM-free) | `npx vitest run --project node packages/core/test/scope.test.ts` | Pass (22 tests, 6 new in `scope-reasons.ts`): a signal first read after disposal carries the reason; children and grandchildren carry it whether their signals were read before or after; so does a child created after disposal and an owned effect's run scope when the owner is disposed (a rerun still ends its run without a reason); the first reason stays (a child's own earlier disposal keeps its reason; a second call changes nothing); without a reason, an `AbortError`. |
| Browser | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/core/test/scope.browser.test.ts` | Pass: the same 6 cases in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit. |
| Regression | `npx vitest run --project node packages/core packages/app packages/state`; the same with `--project browser`, one engine at a time | Node: 260 pass. Browser: Chromium 190 pass, WebKit 188 pass and 2 skipped, Firefox 190 pass. |
| Contracts | `npm run lint:contracts` | Up to date: §4 rows for `scope.signal` and `scope.dispose(reason?)`, the generated `CONTRACTS.md` and `contracts.json`, and the analyzer's core-counter fixture hash. |
| Size | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | All 26 budgets pass: counter 9,532 B, `command` 3,735 B (+12), `router` 6,988 B (+14). |
| Mutants | `node .tmp/mutate.mjs .tmp/mutants-scope-reasons.json` | 6 of 6 killed: the late signal aborted without the reason, a child created after disposal without it, children disposed with the parent without it, an effect run scope without the owner's reason, the reason not stored, and a later call replacing the first reason. |

**Findings:** (1) The eager `scope.signal` reads in `@oddkin/app` (`resource.ts`, `command.ts`, `router.ts`) remain correct, but their comments describe the old behaviour; they can be simplified when that code is next changed. (2) On this machine `localhost` resolves to `::1` first, so the Vitest browser server listens on IPv6 only. In a full `npm test` run Firefox could not open one test session (`NS_ERROR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`; the other 2,199 tests passed), one Firefox-only run ended with an error before any test ran, and two three-engine runs stalled without output and were stopped; each engine alone then passed. The IPv6-only listener is the suspected cause, not yet confirmed or fixed. Earlier entries record the same symptom ("Firefox failed to connect to its browser session").

## 2026-10-21 — Static-host routing (M7 item 897)

Environment as the previous entry. Written on 2026-10-20 in Agent 2's lane (plan item 897 text, core-contracts.md §9 "Static hosts", `packages/app/README.md`, `examples/router` lazy and removed routes with a retry button, base-rooted URLs in its history-mode document) and committed unverified in `1b080f6`; this entry verifies it. No router source changed in this item: the tests exercise behaviour committed in `04e5961` and `63ab2b9`. Contract: [core-contracts.md §9](./architecture/core-contracts.md).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Emulated hosts | `npx playwright test e2e/router-hosting.spec.ts` | Pass: 13 tests in each of Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (39). Four small servers in the spec, each answering missing paths like a class of hosts (no real host deployed): **plain** (the server's 404): history mode cannot start from a deep path, and Back to an in-app entry after another document gets the 404, while hash mode works; **rewrite** (`/* /index.html 200`): deep paths, reloads, and back/forward across documents work, unknown paths answer 200 with the router's not-found; **fallback** (`404.html`, GitHub Pages): deep paths work but answer 404; **documents** (one document per known route, as a static build would write): 200 after a `/x` → `/x/` redirect. Lazy routes: a module failing with a network error, 404, or 503 is the route's error, and Try again (`refresh()`) loads it in place; a module a later deploy removed fails on every host, including the rewrite host's HTML answer, and navigation goes on; a slow lazy load superseded by a link never commits. Also: native escapes from a deep document, a deep link to a fragment in both modes, no page errors. |
| Router regression | `npx playwright test e2e/router.spec.ts` | Pass: 17 tests, 1 skipped (the Firefox modified click, as recorded on 2026-10-19). |

## 2026-10-21 — Workbench shell `<oddkin-workbench>` (M5 items 803, 808–811; 806, 807 remainders)

Environment as the previous entries. Contracts: [workbench.md §2.1](./architecture/workbench.md), [workbench-pages.md §2–§6](./architecture/workbench-pages.md).

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Routes and search (DOM-free) | `npx vitest run --project node packages/workbench` | Pass (91 tests in 10 files). `app.test.ts` on the real `@oddkin/components` registry: every visible page routes to a level that loads that page, hidden pages and a disabled Theme Studio are not routed, legacy routes redirect as `resolveLegacyRoute` does (keeping valid share state, following a moved Theme Studio route), a notice at `/foundations/products` while Products and modes is off, `/` to the first primary destination unless a home page exists; search entries are the registry's, every word must match, titles rank above tag-name matches (one assertion added, below), results capped with the total kept; inline code spans stay text. |
| Element | `npx vitest run --project browser --browser.name=<engine> packages/workbench/test/shell.browser.test.ts` | Pass in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (3 tests each): navigation from the registry in hash mode, `aria-current`, the skip link first, landmarks, grouped cards with counts, focus on the new `h1`, sidebar and back link, "On this page" targets that exist; removing the element returns `scopeStats()` to its baseline and the router stops following the URL; an index that fails to load or has another format, and a failing page, are explained while navigation keeps working. |
| Real browsers | `npx playwright test e2e/workbench.spec.ts` (`oddkin workbench dev` over `tests/fixtures/workbench-oddkin`) | First run, one engine at a time: Chromium 14 and WebKit 14 pass; Firefox 12 pass and 2 fail with `NS_ERROR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` at `page.goto('http://localhost:4175/…')`. After the fix below: Firefox alone 14 of 14 in three consecutive runs, then all three engines together 42 of 42. Covered: navigation with titles, focus, and announcements; deep links, reloads, legacy routes, and not-found; "On this page"; search by keyboard; appearance kept across reloads; the narrow sidebar; the skip link; nine full page loads while the page holds the live-update stream; axe-core WCAG 2.0–2.2 A/AA with no violations on four pages; startup marks; and, on a second dev server over a throwaway project, a content edit applied without a reload, an invalid config reported in a status banner with the page kept, and the banner gone after the fix. |
| Startup | the `records startup timing` test | ms after navigation start, `oddkin-workbench:index` / `:ready`: Chromium 196 / 279, Firefox 929 / 1,033, WebKit 483 / 662 (one sample each, three engines in parallel). |
| Mutants | `node .tmp/mutate.mjs .tmp/mutants-shell.json`; `node .tmp/mutate-e2e.mjs .tmp/mutants-shell-e2e.json` (rebuilds `packages/workbench`, Chromium) | 14 probes, 14 killed. First run 13 of 14: hidden pages routed, no Products notice, no first-destination redirect, any-word search, uncapped results, `aria-current="page"` missing, the application not stopped on disconnect, a failing index not reported, the narrow sidebar left open, the appearance not remembered, an index update ignored, the stale banner kept, the skip link not moving focus. The survivor, ranking worst first, passed because the only ranking assertion compared two summary matches that tie; `app.test.ts` now asserts that titles containing the word come before id-only matches, and it is killed. |
| Pipeline | `npm run typecheck`; `npx prettier --check` on the changed files; `npx vitest run --project browser` | Typecheck passes. Formatting passes. The whole browser project after the Vitest change below: 2,029 passed and 29 skipped in three engines, no errors (one run). |

**Fix (test infrastructure):** `localhost` resolves to `::1` first on this machine, and a server told to listen on `localhost` binds that one address. Firefox sometimes connected over IPv4 and was refused. The Workbench e2e server now listens on `127.0.0.1` (`--host 127.0.0.1` in `playwright.config.ts`; the spec and its second dev server use the same address), and the Vitest browser server too (`test.api.host` in `vitest.config.ts`). This explains the Firefox symptom recorded in the previous two entries and in earlier ones ("Firefox failed to connect to its browser session").

**Findings:** (1) `oddkin workbench dev` defaults to `localhost`, so on machines like this one it is reachable only over IPv6, and Firefox can intermittently fail to open the printed URL or its event stream. A product fix (for example listening on both loopback addresses) belongs to Agent 3's lane; not changed here. (2) The 2026-10-20 live-update failure ("Edited without a reload" never appeared in Firefox) did not reproduce in five Firefox runs. A refused event-stream connection would produce it, but that is not proven. (3) Implemented but untested: refreshing the current page when only its data file changed, reloading when the shell page changed, and the column counts of the overview cards.

## 2026-10-21 — M5 Workbench loopback server, isolated frames, catalog, and basic detail preview

**Environment:** Windows, Node 24.22.1, Vitest 5.0.5, Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium 156, Firefox 157, WebKit 27.2). Agents 1 and 2 had unrelated edits in the shared worktree; they were not included. No full-pipeline run or mutation probe was performed.

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Workbench typecheck | `npx tsc -b packages/workbench` | Pass. |
| Focused Node tests | `npx vitest run --project node packages/workbench/test/protocol.test.ts packages/workbench/test/node-server.test.ts packages/workbench/test/node-inputs.test.ts packages/workbench/test/node-generated.test.ts` | Pass — 31 tests in 4 files. The dev server is reachable over both `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]`; the frame document has the expected response type; frame envelopes, JSON-only payloads, inspect request ids/limits, generated import map, and generated files are checked. |
| Isolated frame browser tests | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/workbench/test/frame.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 6 tests in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit. Covers separate registries and settings, light/dark, actual container-query changes, frame-realm constructed stylesheets, events, error isolation, incompatible registration rejection, generation reload, wrong source/origin, stale messages, and host-scope cleanup. |
| Shell catalog/detail tests | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/workbench/test/shell.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 15 tests in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit. Covers URL-backed catalog search/category filters, grouped counts, no catalog iframe, and a basic detail preview mounted through the published registration entry. |

**Implementation:** The default `localhost` dev server starts IPv4 and IPv6 loopback listeners without exposing the service to other machines. `@oddkin/workbench/protocol` exports DOM-free envelopes, validators, stable example/event/property IDs, and `inspectPreview` request/response types only. `@oddkin/workbench/frame` installs the runtime inside the frame; `createIsolatedPreview()` owns the host frame, message channel, handshake, pending requests, and generation reload. Registration conflicts return `frame.registration.incompatible`; the detail shell mounts a basic preview when a page publishes a registration entry. Catalog search/category state is stored in the URL, and cards do not mount previews. Frames execute trusted project code and are not security sandboxes.

**Remaining:** Full detail/API controls and authored examples (822–831), shell reset/HMR wiring, frame assets/fonts/theme config, fixture/adapter ownership, incompatible-version browser coverage, inspection execution (deferred), and mutation probes. The slice is partial; plan items 822, 831, and 835–838 remain `[~]` with remainders recorded in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.


**Additional type check:** `npx tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json --noEmit` passed, including the frame browser test after replacing its unsupported `Window.eval` use with a frame-owned script.

**Environment:** Windows, Node 24.22.1, Vitest 5.0.5, Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). Other agents had unrelated edits in the shared worktree (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `playwright.config.ts`); they were not included. No full-pipeline run.

| --- | --- | --- |
| App typecheck | `npx tsc -b packages/app` | Pass. |
| Resource, command, router tests | `npx vitest run packages/app/test/resource packages/app/test/command packages/app/test/router` | Pass — 15 files, 95 tests (Node and Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). |
| Size budgets | `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | Pass — app 4,975 B, resource 3,527 B, command 3,735 B, router 6,988 B gzip (budgets 5,376 / 3,840 / 4,096 / 7,680). |
| Mutants | each owner cleanup's `scope.signal.reason` replaced by `undefined`, then the file's tests | 3 of 3 killed: resource (`resource.browser.test.ts` disconnect reason, 3 engines), command (`command.test.ts` "the owner's end cancels the pending run with its reason" and `command.browser.test.ts` disconnect, 3 engines), router (`router.browser.test.ts` "runs as an application service", 3 engines). |

**Change:** core's `Scope` stores the disposal reason and aborts a lazily created controller with it (Agent 1's scope-reasons fix), so the owner cleanups in `@oddkin/app/resource`, `command`, and `router` read `scope.signal.reason` when they run instead of capturing the signal eagerly; the stale comments went with the eager reads. ADR 0006 cites the reference Theme Studio's "Method and limits" section instead of the deleted `docs/reference/design-system-lab-audit.md`.

**Not done:** the other links to the deleted audit note (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §Documents, §Current state, 5d.2, M0, §12.4; workbench.md; workbench-pages.md) belong to shared or other agents' files and are reported in `.tmp/lanes.md`.

| Reproduction | `.tmp` probe: `examples/ai-research` at 320×720 with reduced motion after one streamed answer, `--odd-font-family-sans` and `body` set to Verdana (Bitstream Vera metrics, like DejaVu Sans, which fontconfig gives WebKit for `system-ui` on Ubuntu) | Before: WebKit overflows by 5 px (CI: 4 px). The elements past the viewport are the composer: `odd-prompt-input`'s `form` and `toolbar` at 288 px, sized by the slotted `odd-model-select`, whose `<select>` takes the width of its longest option. The code block (`pre > code`) also extends past the viewport but is a contained scroller and adds no page overflow. Without the font substitution: no overflow. |
| After the fix | the same probe, all three demos × Chromium, Firefox, WebKit × system font and Verdana | 18 of 18 with no page overflow and no element outside the viewport except the code-block scroller. |
| Regression test | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/ai/test/prompt-input.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 30 tests (10 × 3 engines), including the new "a slotted model select with a long option never widens a narrow composer" (a 60-character option in a 15 rem container; host, form, model select, and native select stay inside it with any font). |
| Mutants | five candidate rules removed one at a time, then the new test | The one shipped rule, `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, auto)` on the `odd-model-select` host, killed (3 of 3 engines fail). Four other candidates (`max-inline-size: 100%` on the select, `min/max-inline-size` on the host, `minmax(0, 1fr)` on the composer form, shrinkable slotted toolbar items) survived and were not shipped. |
| Demo e2e | `npx playwright test e2e/ai-demos.spec.ts -g "reflow" --workers=1` | Pass — 9 of 9. A first run in parallel with three browser Vitest suites timed out twice at the streaming wait (line 310, before the overflow assertion), Firefox `ai-agent` and WebKit `ai-research`; both pass alone. `-g "axe"`: 9 of 9 in that first run. |
| Other AI browser suites | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/ai/test/{elements,examples,contract}.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 552 passed, 12 skipped. |
| Size, CSS lint, format | `node scripts/check-size.mjs`; `node scripts/compat/check-css.mjs`; `npx prettier --check` | Pass — `ai:styles` 4,450 B of 4,608 B gzip. The CSS check reported the generated matrix stale (the rule adds compatibility keys: 237 → 238, model-select 17 → 20); regenerated with `--write`, then OK. |

**Change:** `odd-model-select`'s host grid column is `minmax(0, auto)`. With a fixed zero minimum the select's automatic minimum size no longer applies, so it shrinks with its container instead of widening the composer toolbar and form. The layout was fixed, not the test.

**Remaining:** confirmation on GitHub (WebKit on `ubuntu-24.04`) on the next CI run after the user pushes.

## 2026-10-21 — Token scopes across shadow roots and direction (M8 item 909, token side)

**Environment:** Windows, Node 24.22.1, Vitest 5.0.5, Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). Other agents had unrelated edits in the shared worktree (packages/core, packages/workbench, e2e/duplicate-core); they were not included. No full-pipeline run.

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shadow-root scopes | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/tokens/test/scopes-shadow.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 12 tests × 3 engines (36). The `products-density` theme adopted in the document; each probe's colour, surface, link, `space.md`, `layout.gap`, `navigation.item.min-height`, and `navigation.item.padding.inline` equal `resolveToken` for the expected context: a product on the shadow host; mode, product, density, and two profiles on light-DOM ancestors through two nested shadow roots; a density attribute inside a shadow tree ignored without the CSS adopted there and applied with it (also a nested light mode); slotted content taking the mode and product around its slot instead of its light-DOM parent's; a nested `data-odd-theme` resetting an outer product while keeping dark mode. A final test asserts that each pair of probes differs in the axis it tests. |
| Tokens Node tests | `npx vitest run --project node packages/tokens` | Pass — 85 tests, including a guard that no token name names a physical direction. |
| Mutants | 6 mutants in `css.ts`, run against the new test alone | 6 of 6 killed: inverted dark toggle, light scope without its mode closure, scopes declaring only changed tokens, exact instead of `~=` profile matching, and (after moving the theme case under a product, because the first version did not kill it) the theme selector dropped from the base block. |

**Finding:** with the generated CSS adopted inside a shadow root (not the documented setup, which adopts it in the document), a profile on a light-DOM ancestor is lost inside a nested scope in that shadow tree: the scope re-declares base expressions and the profile's re-application selector cannot cross the boundary (`layout.gap` 15 px instead of 10 px in Chromium and WebKit). Pinned by the `profile-lost` / `profile-repeated` cases and documented in theming-and-styling.md §5.3 with the workaround (repeat `data-odd-profile` on the inner scope). A fix would need profile toggles like the mode toggles; not attempted.

**Direction:** no token name contains a physical direction; apps/docs, examples/router, and examples/tokens-css-only use no physical margin, padding, border, or offset properties (searched). Component `dir="rtl"` evidence belongs to the component lane.

## 2026-10-21 — Two copies of `@oddkin/core` on one page (§2.5; M8 duplicate-core behaviour)

**Environment:** Windows, Node 24.22.1, Vitest 5.0.5, Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium 156, Firefox 157, WebKit 27.2), esbuild from the root development dependencies. Other agents were running tests and committing on the same machine; their files were not included.

| Check | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| First observation | `npx playwright test e2e/duplicate-core.spec.ts --project=chromium`, before any core change | 3 of 4 passed. Consumer B's template, bound to consumer A's signal, rendered `1` and still showed `1` after the signal changed to `0`. Readables carry a registered brand (`Symbol.for('oddkin.readable')`), so B's template accepted A's signal, but A's graph tracks reads only for A's own consumers. Nothing was reported. |
| Rejected design | template parts and the six helpers following another copy's readables through `peek()`/`subscribe()` (10 of 10 mutants killed), then `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | `counter` 9,748 B gzip, over its 9,728 B budget; 9,543 B with the adapter taken out of the template parts again: 205 B paid by every application for a case one deduplicated install avoids. Replaced by the development report below and the documented bridge. |
| Core typecheck | `npx tsc -b packages/core` | Pass. |
| New browser tests | `npx vitest run --project browser packages/core/test/foreign-readables.browser.test.ts` | Pass — 11 tests in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (33). A foreign readable is reported once per readable at a child binding, an attribute binding, and by `classMap`, `styleMap`, `live`, `when`, `match`, and `repeat`; it keeps its first value; local readables are never reported; `fromExternal(toExternal(readable))` stays live without a report and releases its subscription with the binding. |
| Core, contract, and import suites | `npx vitest run packages/core/test tests/contracts tests/examples/public-imports.test.ts` | Pass — 52 files, 682 passed, 1 skipped (before the child-binding test was added; that file alone then passed 33 of 33). `packages/core/test/production.test.ts` keeps development messages out of production builds. |
| Contracts | `npm run contracts`; `node scripts/build-contracts.mjs --check` | Pass — 15 sections; new id `contract:core/copies`. |
| End to end | `npx playwright test e2e/duplicate-core.spec.ts` | Pass — 4 tests in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (12): each consumer has its own core; `registry.collision` from B's copy is an `OddkinError` of B's copy only and the first `<odd-button>` stays; the bound signal keeps `1` while the bridged one shows `5`, and B's error handler received exactly one `reactive.readable.foreign`; an effect in B does not rerun for A's signal while one over the bridge does. |
| Size | `node scripts/build-production.mjs`; `node scripts/check-size.mjs` | Pass — `counter` 9,531 B gzip (budget 9,728), `component` 10,808 B (11,264), `list` 11,397 B (11,776); the checks exist only in development builds. |
| Mutants | 11 mutants in `checkForeignReadable` and its callers, Chromium (`.tmp/mutate.mjs`) | 11 of 11 killed: never reporting, reporting local readables, reporting every binding instead of once, and removing the check from each of the eight call sites. |
| Test typecheck | `npx tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json --noEmit` | One error, in another agent's in-progress `packages/workbench/test/panels.browser.test.ts` (line 506, `detail` on `Event`); none in these files. |

**Change:** `checkForeignReadable()` (internal, `@oddkin/core` reactive graph) reports `reactive.readable.foreign` once per readable that carries the brand but belongs to another copy; template child and attribute bindings, `classMap`, `styleMap`, `live`, `when`, `match`, and `repeat` call it in development builds only. Production behaviour is unchanged. [core-contracts.md §5.1](./architecture/core-contracts.md) (contract `core/copies`) states what crosses copies: DOM protocols and the external-store contract; not readables, which are bridged with `fromExternal(toExternal(readable))`; errors by `code`; registration conflicts as `registry.collision`. `examples/duplicate-core` builds the two consumers with esbuild; the spec bundles them in memory and serves them by request interception.

**Remaining:** released packages installed twice at different versions (plan §2.5, item 4), and realms other than Workbench frames.

## 2026-10-21 — Firefox's native accessibility tree through Windows UI Automation (M4 item 724)
